(17.4)

Proof.

The singularity
Q\in Y
has a dual graph

-3
-2
-2
-2
-4

and the extremal neighborhood
(E^{-}\subset\mathcal{Z})
induces a smoothing
\mathcal{Y}\to\Delta
of
Q\in Y
whose Milnor number is
3
by Lemma
17.4
.

Notice that
Q\in Y
is a sandwiched surface singularity. If we choose its sandwiched
(-1)
-curves by one attached to the
(-3)
-curve and the other one attached to the
(-2)
-curve in south position, then we have four incidence matrices

\displaystyle A_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1&0&1&1&1&3\\
1&0&1&1&1&1&3\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1&0&2&2&2\\
1&0&1&2&2&2\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle A_{3}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&2&1&2&2\\
1&1&2&2&2\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle A_{5}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&1&1&1&3\\
1&1&2&2&3\end{bmatrix}

Here
A_{3}^{\prime}
and
A_{5}^{\prime}
correspond to one-parameter smoothings of
Q\in Y
with Milnor number
3
. But one can check via the MMP machinery in the paper that the following
P
-resolutions
P_{3}^{\prime}
and
P_{5}^{\prime}
correspond to
A_{3}^{\prime}
and
A_{5}^{\prime}
, respectively:

P_{3}^{\prime}
-3
-2
-2
-2
-4
P_{5}^{\prime}
-3
-2
-2
-2
-4

According to Kollár–Mori
[
13
, Theorem 13.5]
, the exceptional curve
E^{+}
is irreducible. Therefore the flip of the extremal neighborhood
(E^{-}\subset\mathcal{Z})\to(Q\in\mathcal{Y})
is given by Equation (
17.4
) as asserted.
∎

Lemma 17.6
.

Let
C
be an irreducible curve contained in the central fiber of the extremal neighborhood
(E^{-}\subset\mathcal{Z})
in Lemma
17.5
such that
C\cdot E^{-}=1
and
C
does not pass through the singular point. Let
C^{\prime}
be the proper transform of
C
after the flip along
E^{-}
. Then the curve
C
in the general fiber
Z^{+}_{t}
(
t\neq 0
) degenerates to
C^{\prime}+2E^{+}
in the central fiber
Z^{+}_{0}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
C
in a general fiber
Z^{+}_{t}
degenerates to
C^{\prime}+\beta E^{+}
in the central fiber
Z^{+}_{0}
. By Proposition
5.12
, we have

K_{Z_{0}}\cdot C=K_{Z_{t}}\cdot C=K_{Z^{+}_{t}}\cdot{C}=K_{Z^{+}_{0}}\cdot(C^{\prime}+\beta E^{+})=K_{Z^{+}_{0}}\cdot C^{\prime}+\beta K_{Z^{+}_{0}}\cdot E^{+}.

Let
\pi\colon\widetilde{Z}_{0}\to Z_{0}
and
\pi^{+}\colon\widetilde{Z}^{+}_{0}\to Z^{+}_{0}
be the minimal resolutions, respectively. Let
\widetilde{C}
and
\widetilde{C}^{\prime}
be the proper transforms of
C
and
C^{\prime}
, respectively. Then we have

K_{Z_{0}}\cdot C=K_{\widetilde{Z}_{0}}\cdot\widetilde{C}.

On the other hand, notice that

(\pi^{+})^{\ast}K_{Z^{+}_{0}}=K_{\widetilde{Z}^{+}_{0}}+\frac{1}{3}E_{1}+\frac{2}{3}E_{2}+\frac{2}{3}E_{3}

where
[E_{1},E_{2},E_{3}]
are the exceptional divisors
[2,3,4]
. So we have

K_{Z^{+}_{0}}\cdot C^{\prime}+\beta K_{Z^{+}_{0}}\cdot E^{+}=K_{\widetilde{Z}^{+}_{0}}\cdot\widetilde{C}^{\prime}+\frac{2}{3}+\frac{2}{3}\beta.

But
\widetilde{Z}_{0}\to\widetilde{Z}^{+}_{0}
is a composition of two blowing-ups. Hence

K_{\widetilde{Z}^{+}_{0}}\widetilde{C}^{\prime}=K_{Z_{0}}\cdot C=K_{\widetilde{Z}_{0}}\cdot\widetilde{C}-2

Therefore
\beta=2
.
∎

Proposition 17.7
.

The 6th P-modification
M_{6}
corresponds to the 6th incidence matrix
A_{6}
.

Figure 23.
A P-modification
M_{6}
with a non-T-singularity

Proof.

We present in Figure
23
the process of the semi-stable minimal model program for identifying the picture deformation corresponding to the P-modification
M_{6}
. We briefly explain each steps. In Step 1, we apply the non-usual flip described in Lemma
17.5
. Then we have Step 2. By Lemma
17.6
, we have a degeneration

\widetilde{C}_{1}\rightsquigarrow\widetilde{C}_{1}^{\prime}+2(2)

where
(2)
denotes the
(-2)
-curve in Step 2. We illustrate the degeneration of
\widetilde{C}_{1}
by enclosing them with blue lines. Then, we compute the crepant M-resolution of Step 2. In Step 3, we perform a usual flip on
E^{-}
. Then
\widetilde{C}_{2}
is degenerated, as shown by the orange lines in Step 4. We apply again a usual flip to
E^{-}
in Step 4. Then we resolve all T-singularities. Each
(-1)
-curves will now generate columns of
A_{6}
, which is easily calculable.
∎

Remark 17.8
.

Kollár conjecture for this singularity if we can prove that
\phi_{I}\colon\mathcal{C}(X)\to\mathcal{I}(X)
is injective. But we don’t know how to prove it yet.

]
. Let
(X,p)
be a rational surface singularity whose dual graph of the minimal resolution is given by

.

The decorated curve is given by
(C_{1}\cup C_{2}\cup C_{3}\cup C_{4},\{5,4,4,2\})
and they satisfy the following incidence relations:
C_{i}\cdot C_{j}=2
for
i\neq j
with
i,j\leq 3
and
C_{k}\cdot C_{4}=1
for all
k\leq 3
:

There are six picture deformations of
(X,p)
. In Figure

, we present the decorated curves in a general fiber for each picture deformations. On the other hand, there are also six P-modifications: Fives are P-resolutions, but the sixth is a nonnormal P-modification. We present the dual graphs of five P-resolutions in Figure

.

17.4.1.
A nonnormal P-modification

Kollár
[
12
, Example 6.3.3]
also present a nonnormal P-modification of
(X,p)
. We briefly recall the construction. There are four distinguished points on the central
(-4)
-curve
C
corresponding to the four intersection points with another exceptional curves. We denote the intersection points by
N,E,S,W
according to the direction of the exceptional curves given in the above resolution graph. There is a unique involution
\tau
on
C
such that
\tau(N)=S
and
\tau(E)=W
. We first contract all curves except
C
. Then we have a normal surface
C^{\prime\prime}\subset X^{\prime\prime}
. Then for each
x\in C^{\prime\prime}
we identify
x
and
\tau(x)
to obtain a nonnormal surface germ
g\colon C^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}\to(0\in X)
. Along
C^{\prime}
we have generically normal crossing points. There are also two pinch points corresponding to branch points of the involution
\tau
and two singular points of the form

(xy=0)\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}(1,-1,1)
and
(xy=0)\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{3}(1,-1,1)

Kollár
[
12
, 6.3.3.4]
show that the singularities on
X^{\prime}
have
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein smoothings and
X^{\prime}
is indeed a (nonnormal) P-modification of
X
.

Remark 17.9
.

The picture deformation #6 seems to correspond to the non-normal P-modification. But it is not clear whether one can see the correspondence via the semistable minimal model program.
